- Last 5 plans (10-07 to 10-11): 70, 115, 100, 145, 70 min. 10-11 is back at the phase's median after 10-10's 145, and the split is worth recording because it is the opposite of 10-10's: nothing arrived on disk, nothing had to be reconciled, and the module the plan is named for took minutes - mix.ts is fifty lines. The time went on the three things that are not the feature. A property test that counts its runs, its results and its redrawn positions BEFORE it asserts four empty offender lists. A canvas budget reconciled by re-reading ColourPicker.svelte rather than by citing 10-10's recorded six. And four negative checks, one of which came back green and cost a re-cut of the scan it was supposed to prove.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (10-06 to 10-10): 70, 70, 115, 100, 145 min. 10-10 is the longest of the phase, and the split is the finding rather than the total: the plan's own two tasks were already on disk, so the 145 minutes went on reconciliation, four measured fixes, five negative checks and one full e2e cycle per fix - three builds, three server restarts, and a browser probe for every number quoted
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (10-05 to 10-09): 75, 70, 70, 115, 100 min. 10-09 is the second longest of the phase and roughly a sixth of it went on work no plan can budget for: the previous session was killed with 545 lines uncommitted, so the first fifteen minutes were spent reading nine diffs against the plan and then running the gates the interrupted executor had not - which is where the phone regression turned up, in the one gate task 1's own verify block does not run. The rest split evenly between T1's remaining negative checks and the whole of T2, and the expensive part of T2 was not the forecast but its two accessible twins: an aria-describedby target that exists from first render, and a signed numeral whose permitted character had to be given a scope in the same breath as the permission.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (10-04 to 10-08): 95, 75, 70, 70, 115 min. 10-08 is the longest of the phase so far and the time went where the plan said it would not: not on the lattice, which is forty lines of arithmetic, but on the THREE THINGS THE WIDENING BROKE DOWNSTREAM - a ceiling assertion in a file the plan does not list, a swatch row that renders one element per option, and one consumer outside the widget that read a view index as a knob index. Each was found by a test rather than by inspection, and the last of them only by the full e2e suite. A data widening is cheap; its blast radius is not, and the blast radius is what a plan should enumerate
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (10-03 to 10-07): 75, 95, 75, 70 min for the four that have durations beside them, with 10-07 at about 70. 10-07 is a five-surface plan - two pure modules, two components, a route and two e2e files - and almost none of the seventy minutes went on writing any of them. It went on MEASUREMENT: five of the plan's seven cited line numbers had moved and every one had to be re-found before it could be edited; the union and cross-facet counts were taken through the shipped modules rather than off the assignment table; and two of the plan's own assertions turned out to be wrong and had to be replaced with observations - the gate that was supposed to catch a stale e2e annotation does not read e2e/ at all, and the spec's position for the FOR row is inside a component this phase may not touch. A plan that is line-exact is cheap to execute and expensive to verify, and the verification is the part that is worth the money.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (10-02 to 10-06): 18, 75, ~70, 95, 75 min. 10-06 took about 75 minutes for a data plan, and the cost was not the twenty-nine files: the mechanical re-cut was one script and one minute. It was the three gate specs that broke for RIGHT reasons and had to be re-anchored by measurement rather than by guess - query.spec.ts's three sample tags, filter.spec.ts's tags-only term, and its second-term-narrows pair, which stopped narrowing because the re-cut gives `drums` to exactly the two entries whose descriptions both begin with the word "Nine". A vocabulary change is cheap; every test anchored on a word in it is not.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-08 to 09-10, then 10-01, 10-02, 10-03): 10-03 took about 75 minutes against 10-02's 18, and the ratio is again about what was already measured rather than about what was written. Eighteen files changed and not one of them needed a decision about what to say: the copy was authored in an approved contract and the reservations were arithmetic over a number 10-01 had already taken. What the seventy-five minutes went on was five negative checks rather than four - the fifth because the third came back red where the plan predicted green and the only way to recover the finding was to run it a second way, with the new assertion removed as well as the string restored - plus a full Playwright pass to confirm PREV_E2E, and the discovery that two shipped sentences could not ship at their approved lengths and had to be re-authored under a cap.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-07 to 09-10, then 10-01, 10-02): 10-02 took about 18 minutes against 10-01's 70, and the ratio is the point: Wave 0 spent its time discovering that two of the plan's instruments were wrong, and Wave 2 spent almost none because every number it needed had already been measured and was pasted rather than re-derived. What cost time in 10-02 was not writing CSS - it was six negative checks, one of which needed a scratch commit, a build, an archive listing and a git reset --hard because git archive reads HEAD and no cheaper procedure proves anything.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-06 to 09-10, then 10-01): 10-01 took about 70 minutes, and it is a measurement plan rather than a construction one: three full suites on a clean tree (quick 34 s, sweep 123 s, e2e 110 s), five production builds at 13 s each, four Playwright measurement passes over two engines, and two specs. The expensive part was not writing anything - it was that the plan's CH_PER_LINE instrument was wrong twice and had to be replaced before the number it produced could be trusted.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-05 to 09-09): 41, 55, 62, 48, 41 min. 09-09 is the seventh and last entry wave and the cheapest of the seven, at two configurations rather than three; the two deviations both came from full-suite runs rather than from the per-task gates, which is where an entry wave's remaining risk actually lives.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-03 to 09-07): 78, 62, 41, 55, 62 min. 09-07 is the fifth entry wave and the second most expensive of the five, and the cost is where the last three waves' costs have been: not the writing. Three configurations were authored, measured at three corners each and run against a scripted host, but the wave opened with a MEASUREMENT rather than with an entry — whether `>>` and `&` survive the pinned minifier, run three ways before a line of SWITCH existed, which is what stopped a canonical-form failure being discovered at the gate. FORGE cost the most of the three and none of it was arithmetic: a modal bank is a latch, a latch meets the one firmware bug the simulator cannot reproduce, and the two prescribed mitigations both had to be OBSERVED firing rather than reasoned about. The wave also spent real time on two things that produced no code: reconciling FORGE's required Timer against a declaration the fixture derives, and a negative check whose expected outcome was green.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-02 to 09-06): 42, 78, 62, 41, 55 min. 09-06 is the fourth entry wave and the most expensive of the last three, for a reason worth naming: it is the first wave whose three configurations include two whose whole output is invisible in the browser, so every claim about them had to be made in a header, a bench row and a deferred item rather than shown in a frame — and the one that could be shown, SHUTTLE, had its declared motion corrected by the fixture and its pad rebuilt to make the declaration true.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (09-01 to 09-05): 71, 42, 78, 62, 41 min. 09-05 is the third entry wave and the cheapest of the three, for the reason 09-04 was cheaper than 09-03: every mechanism correction was found by arithmetic before a line was written rather than by a red gate afterwards. Three deviations, none of which cost a re-measurement of a shipped entry, and the only surprise was self-inflicted — the first wall-time plant used the wrong pre-wave commit and gen-og said so in one number.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (07-13, 09-01, 09-02, 09-03, 09-04): 35, 71, 42, 78, 62 min. 09-04 is the second entry wave and it was cheaper than the first for one reason: the mechanism corrections were found before the entries were committed rather than after, by measuring the candidate Lua in a scratch host and reading the rendered frame. Two of the five deviations were found by a gate rather than by a probe - the tune panel's scale vocabulary is enforced by two specs that derive it from the catalog - and those two cost a rewrite of one knob and a re-measure of one entry.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (07-12, 07-13, 09-01, 09-02, 09-03): 47, 35, 71, 42, 78 min. 09-03 is an entry wave and the writing was not its cost: three configurations authored, measured and rendered through the pinned minifier is perhaps a third of it, and the rest is four measured corrections to the plan's own mechanism - each one found by running the thing and looking at the frame rather than by reading the source - plus two frames regenerations, a before-and-after build taken by checking the pre-wave catalog out and putting it back, and four negative checks.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (07-11 to 07-13, then 09-01, 09-02): 27, 47, 35, 71, 42 min. 09-02 wrote one new spec and rewrote three, and its cost was not the writing: six negative checks, each observed red or deliberately green on a named test and each restored byte-identical, plus four full quick runs and a sweep.
- Previous note, retained: Last 5 plans (07-10 to 07-13, then 09-01): 78, 27, 47, 35, 71 min. 09-01 is Phase 9's infrastructure plan and it is long for one reason: six full-suite measurements on a clean tree plus four more around the sweep move, and the sweep alone is 110 s a run.
- Trend: down. 07-13 took about 35 minutes (measured from 07-12's docs commit to its own, because no start stamp was taken) for one runbook, two documents amended, eight deferred items and one full gate run, and the code was zero by rule. The wall time was the gate itself (check, lint, build, quick, sweep and e2e in sequence, about five minutes, every number first time and none re-run - item 12's load-sensitive test did not fire at 1.7 GB free) and the runbook, which took longer than the gate because every Passes-when cell quotes the string install-copy.ts or session-copy.ts renders, and because two of its rows had to be redesigned against the store's own semantics rather than transcribed from the research: row E runs in one tab because keptThisSession is a field of the page load (found by reading install.svelte.ts, recorded as item 29), and row F leaves a memory-only try-on on the module before the browser is quit, because the research's row F as written - after row E's final PUT BACK - would have restored an original over an identical original and proved nothing about the record. One lesson worth keeping: a runbook row that asks for a number must say where on the page the number is, and the check is to look - the plan said refetchRounds was on the probe and the probe shows pacing only, so the row reads the count from the steps list instead.
- Previous note, retained: up. 07-12 took 47 minutes for one e2e file extended by five tests (721 -> 1780 lines) and no source file, and for once the code was not the small part: the four real-page tests were green first time, but the tagged degrade test's desktop run found a one-pixel horizontal scroll at 1280 that took two diagnostic passes and two dropped assertion models to name honestly (item 20), the Task 1 commit had to be carved from a file authored in one pass so it held Task 1's work, and quick was run three times against item 12's load-sensitive timeout on a machine at under 2 GB free before being recorded as it read.
- Previous note, retained: down. 07-11 took 27 minutes for one component amended (DeviceDetails +102/-10), one structural test and one e2e file extended, and the code was again the small part: every constant the interfaces block named existed under its spelling in session-copy and the install store, and the only compile error was a rune's placement. The wall time was three builds (the good tree, the RAM-legs-only mutation, the restore), one scratch Playwright probe whose two tests took 5.6 s against a static server on 4174 and recorded all four header observations in one walk each - the drawer opened mid-leg by un-choosing the panel with history.back() inside the page, because the real page forbids an open drawer while the panel is chosen - one negative on a served mutated build and two in node and under the shipped config, every restore hashed, and two wrangler runs that both passed first time (11, then 83).
- Previous note, retained: up, and the number is two sessions' wall time. 07-10 took 78 minutes from 07-09's docs commit to its own for four components amended (TryOnDevice +257/-151, KeepOnDevice +208/-57, ChosenPanel +119, Coverflow +16) and one structural test, across a session that exited after its third task commit and a second that reconciled the record. The code was again the small part; the wall time was measurement and honesty twice over - the first executor's served-build walk of nine measurements and four negative checks, and the second executor's decision to treat the three commit messages as claims: a clean build served on 4174, every measurement re-observed with a MutationObserver on the record, the retained literal and the dropped Escape guard observed on one mutated build and the space-between row on another, three restores hashed against HEAD, and a full two-project e2e run whose first attempt was a harness drop (37 x Could not connect to server) and whose rerun read 83 green.
- Previous note, retained: down. 07-09 took 33 minutes for three components (246, 314 and 228 lines) and two structural tests, and again the code was the small part: the interfaces block named every prop, testid and string, and the store's public surface from 07-07 fitted it without a single rename. The wall time was measurement and honesty - two served builds (11 s each), a scratch Playwright spec that had to move inside the repository to resolve @playwright/test, a busy-state measurement re-planned once when a dropped-acknowledgement script let the Timer land and the put-back correctly waited on a store-leg heartbeat, a rig measurement that needed one more ZONA beat because the fold restarts at connected (deferred item 13), and a planned negative check that stayed green in its additive form and earned the floor rule a strengthening. The quick suite's one failure (Phase 8's lua-entries.spec.ts under load) cost two full runs and is deferred rather than rerun a third time.
- Previous note, retained: up. 07-08 took 45 minutes for two fixture files and one test file (274 and 721 lines new, the shim +81) and a 264-line probe, and the code was again the small part. The wall time was three things: a round-trip self-check that had to be redesigned when it showed the session never writes (the plan's self-check could not drive the Node half from the session probe before task 2 started the store, so one raw CONFIG/FETCH was pushed from the page instead); the first install run failing all six on a plan-table testid collision; and the full suite failing seven Phase 6 tests the moment the layout started the store - a planned change landing on tests the plan did not list, fixed by giving those tests the answering module and the by-class count SAFE-01 now means. Two full two-project runs at --workers 3: the first tripped the inherited pre-hydration key press (deferred item 8), the second was 83 green.
- Previous note, retained: level. 07-07 took 26 minutes for +456 lines of store and +898 of spec against 07-06's 33 on the same seams, and again the code was the small part: every member the interfaces block promised existed under its spelling except the sketch's two slips (T.storePage for a protocol descriptor; a generation check ahead of the AbortedError branch that 07-06 had already found could never land lost). The wall time was observation. The first spec run failed five tests for two harness reasons that are worth one sentence each: a predicate checked synchronously before an action's first await has moved the phase reads the OLD phase, and a lockstep clock that steps before the yield that lets the queue's microtasks run stamps every sentAt one step late - both cured by one macrotask yield before the clock first moves. The second run failed two assertions written against the wrong model (a strict inequality where the send shares the feed's clock reading; a fake's flash that stores before its acknowledgement is dropped). Seven negative checks each applied-run-restored with a hash comparison; one could not go red as planned because a second guard stands in front of the first, and it was recorded rather than forced. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous trend, retained: up, and for a reason worth naming. 07-06 wrote the phase's largest file so far (641 lines of store, 783 of spec) in 33 minutes, and the code itself was about twelve because every seam had a name (the session's six, the queue's request / deliver / abort / sendImmediate, snapshot.ts's five functions, install-copy's sentences) and the interfaces block was close to complete. The wall time was, again, measurement and observation: the header's eight prose paragraphs, the scan script before the spec existed, two lint findings that each pointed at a specifier rule from the other side (SvelteDate; an unread private field), one bug in the plan's own catch ordering that could never have landed `lost`, and five negative checks each applied-run-restored with a hash comparison - one of which said the true thing in the wrong words (a phase that moved rather than a write that happened) and earned a tightened assertion. Two lessons worth keeping: when a plan's sketch orders a generation guard before an error-type check, trace the event that bumps the generation to the event that raises the error before copying the order; and when a fake has no injection point, tee it rather than replace it, so its faults are still there for the next plan. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: flat and low. 07-05 added one emit and one parameter to a function Phase 5 had already shaped, one prop to each of three components, and one spec that re-derives a table the research had already printed, and the code was under ten minutes because every shape had a home (land() for the emit, the two stale sites for the withdrawal, onbudget's thread for the prop, sequence.spec.ts's rig() for the wire). The wall time was measurement and observation: a pre-edit re-measure of quick, four negative checks each applied-run-restored with a hash comparison, two lint rules that fire on the same unread prop from opposite sides, and one negative check that stayed green and earned a rewrite - the plan's reserve mutation cannot move an independent costOf(), only the tuner's own meter, so the pin was restated against the meter the visitor sees. Two lessons worth keeping: read the fixture's spec header before trusting a plan's fixture (ladder.spec.ts had already measured that tpad offers no step); and a negative check that stays green is the most useful result a negative check can return, because it names the half of the pin that was missing. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: flat and low. 07-04 added six members to a file Phase 6 had already shaped and six literals to a copy module Phase 6 had already gated, and the code was under ten minutes because every seam had a named home (the fold pump for the fan-out, #teardown for the closed event, #say for announce, the permissionDeclined pattern for the modifier). The wall time was measurement and observation: a pre-edit re-measure of quick, the four-specifier and needle scan run before the spec existed, a scratch script importing the copy module under Node 24's native type stripping to measure six lengths and the export walk before and after (four of the provisional figures typed into comments were wrong and the script caught them), eight negative checks each applied-run-restored with a hash comparison, then check, lint, quick and sweep through check-counts. One lesson worth keeping: a lint rule (prefer-svelte-reactivity) and a plan rule (four static specifiers) can point in opposite directions on the same line, and the tree already held the answer - two components carry the same directive with a reason - so the fix was a precedent to follow rather than a decision to make. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: flat and low. 07-03 wrote two pure modules and two specs from a fully specified interfaces table and an approved copy contract, and the code itself was under fifteen minutes because every shape had a precedent in the tree (return.ts for the store, session-copy.ts for the copy module, the SAMPLES walk and the stripped-source scan for the specs). The wall time was measurement and observation: a pre-edit re-measure of quick, a scratch script that imported the module and matched 42 literals against the contract, eight negative checks each applied-run-restored with a hash comparison (one of them a full svelte-check run for the compile-error case), then check, lint, quick and sweep through check-counts. One lesson worth keeping: the compile-error negative caught a type error in the spec being written that Vitest could never have shown, because Vitest strips types - a predicate narrowing Object.entries of a namespace import is rejected by the checker and silently accepted by the runner, so a spec that only ever ran under Vitest would have shipped three svelte-check errors. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: 07-02 generalised one function, removed one throw, widened one responder and wrote five tests, and the code was under ten minutes because every shape it needed already existed (sendConfig, the queue's settle, configNackFrame, the rig() helper). The wall time was six negative checks each applied-run-restored with a hash comparison, plus check, lint, quick and sweep. Two facts cost a probe each and were worth it: the pinned package decodes an addressed frame's DX/DY as 0,0 and a broadcast's as -127,-127 (so the responder can compare them to the state's own address directly), and every existing consumer of zonaResponder addresses 0,0 to a state at 0,0 (so adding firmware's IS_ME rule for CONFIG broke nothing unedited). One lesson worth keeping: a rig responder is only honest if the single-module responder already answers by address - a fan-out over modules that all answer everything models three ZONAs sharing one RAM, not three modules on one cable. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: 07-01 was down, and for the reason 06-01 was cheap: 07-01 discovered almost nothing. Two source edits against a module Phase 2 shaped (one descriptor, one helper, one step id), two tests, one widening, three observed negatives - the code was a few minutes. The wall time was measurement: check, quick, sweep, a per-file JSON run, build and Playwright at --workers 3 on the clean tree before the first edit, then quick and sweep again through check-counts at the end. Every baseline equalled 06-14's closing block and no number was adjusted; format-parity did not flake and nothing was re-run. One lesson worth keeping: when a plan's prose and its own code snippet disagree (fetch-serial 'after fetch-timer' versus second in the array), the snippet plus the acceptance criterion plus the research's stated timing settle it, and the SUMMARY says which was followed and why. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: 06-14 was flat. 06-14 wrote two documents and ran the phase gate once: check, lint, build, quick (69 / 724 = BASE + 3 / + 33), sweep (3 13), e2e at --workers 3 (77 = BASE_E2E 61 + 16), every number first time and none re-run - the two flake-prone spots (format-parity under load, the wrangler death at eight workers) did not fire. The wall time was the runs themselves (about nine minutes of gate plus a sequential timing pass for docs/TESTING.md's wall column, because the doc's rule is that every figure is observed), plus the runbook, which took longer than the code of most plans in the phase because every Passes-when cell quotes the exact strings the header and the disclosure show, and each of those was read from session-copy.ts and transport.ts rather than remembered. One lesson worth keeping: a runbook row that says what the page will show is only worth writing if the writer checked the string against the source that renders it - the plan's own row E said the header "returns to NO ZONA" after FORGET THIS ZONA, and the spec's S7 says CONNECT ZONA with no caption until the reload, which is what the runbook now says. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: 06-13 was flat and low. 06-13 wrote five browser tests and no probe: the shipped chrome was driven through the harness's own web server rather than a scratch static server, so every measurement was a test run and no build was made outside Playwright. The wall time was eight harness starts (two task runs, one webkit run, one re-run after the focus fix, two ordering mutations, one double-speak mutation, the full suite) at 15-70 s each, plus the quick, sweep, check and lint gates. Two facts cost a run each and were harness facts: the identity label's spaces are non-breaking entities, so a substring match folds whitespace first; and a plain in-budget knob turn is silent by Phase 5's contract, so the tuning region is made to speak with RESET ALL. One fact cost a run and was a product one: the S6 auto-open recorded the body as the element to return focus to, because the click's S3 phase disables and blurs the slot - found only because the test clicked the real button through the real states rather than toggling a probe, which is the argument for this plan existing. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: 06-12 was back down. 06-12 rewrote one component inward and grew one gate by a test, and the code itself was under fifteen minutes; the rest was five builds (task 1, its negative, task 2, its negative, the restored tree via the e2e web server) and four served-build probe passes plus the shipped degrade file and the full suite. Two probe corrections cost a build-free rerun each (a count taken inside the note twin's 160ms fade; a busy flag planted on a fresh port where the session's adopted-port path reopens port 0), and both are recorded as harness facts rather than product ones. The previous notes are retained below.
- Previous note, retained: 06-10 was up again, and it earned it. 06-10 built two components and a structural gate but its wall time was almost all measurement and the harness: five site builds (task 1's slot, task 1's two mutations, the corrected label, task 2's harness with two DeviceDetails mounts, and the restored builds), ten browser measurement passes against a scratch static server on 4174, and ten negative checks each applied-run-restored. Two lessons worth keeping. First, Svelte's whitespace handling is part of the contract: the plan's interface showed the identity tail with plain spaces, and plain spaces vanish at element boundaries — the label rendered `ZONA·fw1.5.5·page3` on the served build, and the `{" "}` that would fix it is lint-banned, so `&nbsp;` was the only form both tools accept, found only because the label was actually measured rather than assumed. Second, a plan's negative can name a defect its own probe cannot stage: task 1's "tail missing on first paint at 1280" only exists in S4, which is never a first-paint state, so the same mutation's resize-staleness was recorded instead — a real defect of the intended change, not a fake. Feeding a rig through the shim also cost a detour: synthetic heartbeats need the trailing LF terminator the frame scanner scans for, and the fresh post-connect fold needs the ZONA's own heartbeat re-fed before `identify()` returns.
- Previous note, retained: 06-09 was up, and the plan said it would be. 06-09 warned it was "longer than most plans in this phase, and deliberately not split", and it was: three tasks that each end in a build plus a served measurement, five builds in all (the mount, the two-mutation build twice - once to fix a measurement filter that `display: none` slipped past - the restored build, and the layout's negative twice), three full e2e runs (one flake, one wrangler death, one clean at `--workers 3`), and a Rule 2 fix in the middle because the plan's module-scope negative was unobservable on Node 24 and the right answer was a guard rather than a deferred item. The code itself - the coalescer, two components, six lines of layout - was about a quarter of the wall time. Two lessons: a measurement filter is part of the measurement and deserves its own negative (the first never-both count was an artefact of a filter, caught because the mutated numbers looked too tidy); and a plan's negative that depends on a runtime fact ("Node has no navigator") should state the runtime, because that fact changed at Node 21 and the check went silently green.
- Previous note, retained: 06-08 was flat. Three leaf components with no state and no session in them is about the cheapest shape a plan takes, and the code itself was under five minutes; the rest was measurement and the harness. Each of the two browser negatives cost a build plus a served measurement (~25 s), the same price 06-07 recorded, and the temporary probe mount added one build and one hash round trip. What cost more than it should was the e2e gate: two full runs died at the WebServer with wrangler's `Network connection lost.` before a third at `--workers 3` passed, roughly four minutes of wall time for a fact about the harness rather than the code (deferred item 7). One lesson worth keeping: a plan's negative check must name a test that actually reads the file being mutated - the hex negative there named `identity.spec.ts`, which reads two files and neither is a component, so it was green and could only be recorded, not observed red.
- Previous note, retained: 06-06 was down, and for the reason 06-01 was cheap: the ground was already measured. 06-06's three files had a reference implementation each (the research's shim, the four shipped probes, the degrade block's precondition pattern), and the one thing the reference got wrong - a `Proxy` over an Event, which `dispatchEvent` refuses - was found by the self-check the plan required rather than by a debugging session. The cost that was new was four Playwright starts (each a build plus `wrangler dev`), because every negative on an e2e file needs one; the runner for non-vitest negatives was the only tooling written.
- Previous note, retained: 06-05 wrote no new test and no new source — three widenings of shipped assertions — and its cost was entirely observation: eight mutations applied and restored by the runner, two of them deliberately green, and two report figures that had to be surfaced through a temporary failing assertion because vitest swallows `console.log` here. The one discovery (three probe pages spelling a sibling's path) cost four one-line comment rewrites and one paragraph.
- Previous note, retained: 06-04 finished the session in the same 24 minutes 06-03 took to start it, and for the same reason: the vocabulary was already named (`#attachListeners`, `#adopt`, `#teardown`, `#onTransportClosed`, `#now`) and every fixture existed. The cost that was new was the negative-check discipline — six mutations, each applied and restored by a scratchpad runner — and the fake-timer design for the watchdog, which needed one decision (fake `setTimeout` alone, poll through `setImmediate`) rather than a debugging session. The tool-caught issues were three unused private members at the first lint, which is what moved the watchdog into task 1.
- Previous note, retained: 06-03 wrote the phase's largest file (553 lines of session, 589 of spec) in 24 minutes, because 06-01 and 06-02 had already measured the ground and placed the copy: the four static specifiers were verified by reading, the taxonomy was already import-free, and every fixture the spec needed (the capture, `heartbeatFrame`, `FakeTransport.fromCapture`) already existed. The eight tests and four negative checks were green or red on the first run each; the four tool-caught issues (a readonly array, a dead assignment, the chunk's third specifier, the vacuous barrel check) each cost one run.
- Previous note, retained: 06-01 is the cheapest plan since 05.1-03, and for the same reason 05.1-11 was cheap: it discovered almost nothing. Two four-line source edits against modules Phase 2 and Phase 4 had already shaped, three tests, three observed negative checks. The expensive half was measurement — a full check, quick, sweep, build and Playwright run on a clean tree before any edit — which is wall time rather than thinking.

## Accumulated Context

### Roadmap Evolution
- Phase 12.1 inserted after Phase 12 (2026-09-11): Gradient touch - the sensor is not under the LEDs; calibration probe, bilinear G(x,y,b) replacing Q's hit-test, every finger-drawing entry re-fitted; must land before 13-14 (URGENT)

- Phase 12 added 2026-09-10: **Touch Framework** - the user second bench round (`11-bench-corrections/BENCH-2026-09-10.txt`, copied into the phase directory) found EUCLID, STEPS, LUMEN and MORPH unfixed on hardware after fixes the simulator measured as correct, and asked for a finger-to-LED interaction framework. The system element - three events, each 908, and system setup runs before touch setup per `grid_ui.c:986` - makes it a library rather than an idiom. Phase 11 closed the same day with its checkpoint answered by that bench round.
- Phase 11 added 2026-09-09: **Bench Corrections** - the user tested all thirty-six configurations on a real ZONA and the notes are kept verbatim at `.planning/phases/11-bench-corrections/BENCH-2026-09-09.txt`. Nine removals take the catalog to twenty-seven; the nine preset definitions move out of `src/vendor/botor/_pad.ts` into HANGAR so the catalog stops depending on another project to change its own contents, while the vendored compiler and simulator stay untouched.
- Roadmap defect fixed in the same commit: the `## Phases` checklist stopped at Phase 9, so **Phase 10 had no line there for the whole redesign**, and `gsd-tools phase add` filed Phase 11 detail block after `## Verification Note` rather than inside `## Phase Details`. Both corrected.

### Decisions

Decisions are logged in PROJECT.md Key Decisions table.
Recent decisions affecting current work:

- [10-11]: **The two candidates are text and only the results are pads.** 11.6 costs MIX TWO at four canvases beside the hero and the picker one result - six on the worst entry, which is inside the front door seven. A mini for THIS ONE and one for THAT ONE would be eight, the same eight one-picker-per-panel was bought to avoid, and THIS ONE is already running as the hero six centimetres up the same panel. Words are also the only form in which two configurations can be DIFFERENCED at a glance, which is what the slots are for. Reconciled by re-reading ColourPicker.svelte here rather than by citing 10-10 recorded number.
- [10-11]: **A metaphor scan matches by stem, because a whole-word one passed its own negative check.** The plant was Breeds half its settings from each and the scan stayed green - breeds is not breed - and would have stayed green on breeding, parents, mutated and children too. A metaphor arrives inflected far more often than bare. Nine roots, each with its reason, and the FOUND FORM in the failure message beside the root it came from.
- [10-11]: **Randomness that a component can see is randomness a test must be able to seed.** mixIndices takes rng with NO DEFAULT, stricter than surpriseIndices, which may fall back to Math.random because it predates T4. There is not one Math.random CALL anywhere under src/lib outside a spec, and tune-ui.spec.ts asserts it over comment-stripped code for every file under src/lib/ui/ - the form that cannot be satisfied by deleting a paragraph.
- [10-11]: **Non-destructive is asserted as a count.** ontake has exactly one call site, and roll() is asserted not to reach it by slicing the source between the two function declarations. A claim that the current state survives until a result is clicked is a claim about call sites, and call sites can be counted.
- [10-11]: **A component is not mounted when mounting it would take a decision the spec has not taken.** MIX TWO waits on the same SimHost hop the picker result pad waits on - Coverflow.svelte, which this phase promises not to edit - AND on a reservation term: TuningRegion height is 194 + 48r + 66w + 196p - 4, asserted twice, and 10-UI-SPEC gives MIX TWO no term in it. A block whose height changes when four results appear is the reflow that reservation exists to prevent. Deferred item 8.
- [10-11]: **No disabled reason was invented for a control that has none.** MIX TWO needs a second candidate and can be disabled; 13.4 gives it four strings and no fifth, where SURPRISE ME has its 53-character sentence for the same situation. A component that writes its own sentence is the drift copy.ts exists to prevent, so the gap is recorded with two ways out rather than closed by hand. Deferred item 9.
- [10-09]: **A second channel instead of a ninth colour.** A held knob is signalled by the WORD on its toggle changing HOLD to HELD and by its default marker changing from a 2px --color-line-soft dot at the default to a 2px --color-line bar spanning the selected option. Two non-colour channels, so 10-UI-SPEC 7.2's reserved list stays at eight and the accent census over the seven tuning components is 14 before and 14 after - and the census is now an ASSERTION with all eight entries quoted in its failure message rather than a script somebody has to remember to run.
- [10-09]: **A memo hit is not debounced, and that asymmetry is the design.** The forecast waits out the same COMPILE_DEBOUNCE_MS a recompile waits on a MISS, so a sweep across a word row schedules one compile per 120 ms; a HIT publishes on a microtask, so a re-hovered option gets its ghost back with no delay. One timing constant, two behaviours, and no ghost that lags the pointer.
- [10-09]: **A permitted character arrives with its scope in the same commit.** U+2212 MINUS SIGN is the fifth typographic character the site ships, permitted in forecastDelta and nowhere else, and copy.spec.ts asserts BOTH halves: that the delta writes it rather than a hyphen-minus, and that it appears exactly once in the comment-stripped code of all of src/. The spec names the glyph by escape so the file cannot defeat its own assertion. A permitted character with no scope is how a copy contract loosens one glyph at a time.
- [10-09]: **Every flexible grid track in a knob row is minmax(0, 1fr).** A track's automatic minimum size is its content's min-content width, so the moment the lock's column turned the row into a grid the options row stopped wrapping and pushed knob-rack to scrollWidth 267 against clientWidth 245 on the phone project - a breach of the tuning region's outright prohibition on horizontal scroll, invisible to check, lint and 802 unit tests. The measurement and the failing assertion are written beside the rule, because the natural tidy-up is to simplify it back to 1fr.
- [10-09]: **The forecast is offered where an option is a real element.** Word rows and swatch rows give the platform something to hover and focus; a rail is one range input whose focus is the CURRENT value, and hand-writing a pointer-to-detent mapping for it would be a second unverified copy of the platform's own hit-testing. Five of the twelve knob kinds are rails unconditionally, so the gap is counted rather than glossed, and 10-10's picker is the mapping that closes it.

- [10-06]: **A term earns a chip because it is a member of a declared facet, never because two entries happen to carry it.** The derived row moved every time an entry landed and three quarters of it matched a single card; a closed vocabulary in facets.ts cannot drift, needs no disclosure, and makes an entry that does not fit evidence that the VOCABULARY is wrong.
- [10-06]: **OR within a facet, AND across, and the OR half is required rather than conventional.** FOR gives every entry exactly one term, so under a pure AND any second FOR chip returns zero and immediately disables itself. A facet whose second click is always dead is not a facet.
- [10-06]: **No singleton is mapped through the legacy ?tag= table.** A tag that sat on one entry MEANT that card, so sending ?tag=looper to a nine-entry modulation chip shows a grid its author never saw. All fifty-five are keys and thirty-three are explicitly undefined, so a shipped-but-unmappable tag is distinguishable from a word that was never a tag.
- [10-06]: **Arithmetic that can only be done once is REPORTED, not asserted.** After the re-cut nothing in the repository knows who carried `hypnotic`, so a test claiming to check the fold rule would be checking nothing; the derivation's output is in the summary and RETIRED_VOCABULARY bounds the table instead.
- [10-05]: **A configuration that paints nothing UNTIL it is touched gets a finger; a configuration that paints nothing WHEN touched gets a sentence.** Trackpad lights 0 of 81 cells under five different gestures because its draft enables no LED layer at all. The alternative is 10-UI-SPEC 9.3's rejected option (a), and the ruling itself says which promise wins when the two collide: the firmware supplies every lit pixel, so HANGAR must not invent one.
- [10-05]: **An exemption is a named row with a reason, never a flag.** gen-og.mjs's non-dark gate used to exempt any restsBlack entry, which would have re-exempted an entry whose demo path was deleted; it now exempts only DARK_BY_CONSTRUCTION, and the negative check that removes ETCH's path goes red naming etch in two places instead of passing.
- [10-05]: **One TouchSampler per demo entry, not a bigger MAX_CONTACTS.** Measured on a shared sampler: three of the visitor's five fingers accepted, ZERO delivered to the hero, and three of four demo cards receiving nothing because their paths reuse pointer ids. All three failure modes are silent - the pointer is simply never captured.
- [10-05]: **A demo card is active because it IS a demo card, not only while its sampler has samples.** The term 10-UI-SPEC 9.3 names stops the loop between two gestures, and nothing but a tick can queue the next sample, so a demo that stops between gestures never resumes.
- [10-03]: **A literal that exceeds a measured cap is SHORTENED and the cap NEVER rises.** A cap widened to admit its own string stops reserving anything, and the reservation is the whole reason the cap exists. HONESTY_READY and tryOnBudgetReason's worst form were both authored at 90 in the approved contract against HONESTY_CAP 86 and both ship at 85.
- [10-03]: **An amendment that a gate cannot verify is recorded as a row asserted from BOTH sides, never as an exemption.** install-copy.spec.ts's AMENDED_BY_MEASUREMENT requires the contract's form to be present in an approved contract AND over the cap, so deleting the row is red on the shipped string and faking it is red on the contract's.
- [10-03]: **A named amendment asserts the ABSENCE as well as the presence.** Every retired export is held out of Object.keys(copy) by name. Proved necessary rather than assumed: with SHARE_QUIET_LINE re-exported and the absence assertion removed, the entire quick suite is green at 76 / 787.
- [10-03]: **A copy gate that reads its contract from disk reads EVERY approved contract that amends it**, and asserts each was loaded, is the document it claims to be, and is approved - a failed read is a gate that passes everything.
- [10-03]: **SAFE-01 ships as form 1**, SAFE_NOTE on the control: 35 characters, unconditional, never a sizing twin, in every state on both surfaces that carry the primary. Forms 2 and 3 are costed at four files each in 10-03-SUMMARY.md, and the user rules.
- [10-03]: **A site-wide invariant scan names its expected non-offenders rather than excluding a directory.** Z-08's walk over src/ carries /dev/type/ as a named row with its reason - the probe's copy of the sentence is the string 10-01's line-box occupancies were measured against, and rewriting it would falsify the record.
- [10-01]: **CH_PER_LINE is 43, not 46.** Measured as the minimum occupancy of a full line box over thirty-six full lines in both engines, because a cap has to be a promise about strings not yet written rather than a description of the ones that exist. 47 would also never under-reserve on today's corpus and was rejected for exactly that reason. §12.1's premise is refuted: Inter is 2.35 per cent wider than Quicksand at 16px, not narrower.
- [10-01]: **FONT_SRC is §5.1 candidate (b)**, the relative url() into node_modules, proved under vite build and vite preview at the probe route's depth AND at src/app.css's. The second proof took a perturbation of a file this plan may not change, reverted byte-identical with the sha256 stated before and after - because the ../ count differs by location and app.css goes through @tailwindcss/vite while a component <style> does not.
- [10-01]: **A font that ships in the artefact is a production dependency.** @fontsource-variable/inter went into dependencies rather than devDependencies, against the plan's own -D, because /dev/type/ is prerendered and deployed and license-checker-rseidelsohn runs --production. licence-notices.spec.ts made the argument by going red.
- [10-01]: **A source scan matches a CSS rule on the rightmost compound of its selector and a declaration on its property name, never on a body substring.** Coverflow.svelte:1013 is the reason and the test: .stage.measured .slot declares transition with filter and opacity inside it, and it is legal.
- [10-01]: **A spec whose loop can be empty proves its own matcher fires before it trusts it.** expect.requireAssertions caught two font-assets tests making no assertion against an empty allowlist; the fix is not a dummy assertion but a matcher self-test, because a regular expression that matches nothing is a gate that is green forever.
- [Roadmap]: Two independent tracks after Phase 1 — hardware (2 → 6) and pure/no-hardware (3 → 4 → 5) — joining at Phase 7. Parallel execution is real, not cosmetic.
- [Roadmap]: The walking skeleton (Phase 2) runs before any framework work because it is the only phase whose failure invalidates the core value; its write is a provable no-op on the user's own hardware.
- [Roadmap]: Snapshot + PUT BACK ships in the same phase as the first real write (Phase 7). Store-to-flash is a step inside that phase, strictly after audition and PUT BACK both work.
- [Roadmap]: The fidelity oracle is established in the vendoring phase (Phase 3), before the simulator is trusted to render anything.
- [Roadmap]: GPLv3 licence and the exact grid-protocol pin land in Phase 1 — free now, miserable to retrofit.
- [Phase 01]: sv@0.17.0 has no --no-git-check and no -C/--cwd flag, and rejects cfTarget for non-Cloudflare adapters; the scaffold command is 'sv create . --template minimal --types ts --no-install --no-dir-check --no-download-check --add prettier eslint tailwindcss=plugins:none playwright vitest=usages:unit sveltekit-adapter=adapter:static'
- [Phase 01]: Kit config lives only in vite.config.ts with FLAT options inside the sveltekit() argument; no svelte.config.js and no kit: {} wrapper anywhere
- [Phase 01]: sv 0.17.0 emits src/routes/layout.css, not src/app.css; HANGAR uses src/app.css imported from +layout.svelte as ../app.css
- [Phase 01]: .planning/ and CLAUDE.md are Prettier-ignored but never gitignored, so npm run lint stays green without rewriting tracked planning documents
- [Phase 01]: The D-15 vendor canary asserts formatter-output parity with grid-editor (HANGAR prettier stdout === grid-editor prettier stdout, byte for byte), not `prettier --check` cleanliness of a copied BOTOR file — Three of the four grid-editor zona files are not Prettier-clean upstream (pad-sim.ts 6 hunks), so the check-based form was permanently red for a reason outside HANGAR control; the D-15 property is a clean re-sync diff, which holds iff the two formatters agree
- [Phase 01]: The canary runs grid-editor own node_modules/prettier/bin/prettier.cjs with cwd = the BOTOR root, in stdout mode only; the sibling repo is never written to — Catches a Prettier version or plugin divergence between the repos instead of assuming parity
- [Phase 01]: THIRD-PARTY.md and licenses/ are committed, not gitignored as research suggested — npm run build must be reproducible from a clean clone without first running the licence checker, the source archive should carry the notices it references, and the staleness spec can then read them directly instead of shelling out
- [Phase 01]: The source archive filename carries the FULL 40-character SHA (the __COMMIT_SHA__ contract) while the archive internal prefix uses the short SHA — The plan-04 footer links at /source-{__COMMIT_SHA__}.tar.gz and __COMMIT_SHA__ is git rev-parse HEAD verbatim; a short-SHA filename would never resolve
- [Phase 01]: scripts/postbuild.mjs deletes any pre-existing build/source-*.tar.gz before writing the current one — Two archives served side by side leaves a visitor unable to tell which source matches the bundle they ran - the precise silent failure GPLv3 section 6(d) exists to prevent
- [Phase 01]: npm uninstall does not restore package-lock.json byte-exactly on this machine (tabs become two spaces), so any npm install/uninstall round trip needs an explicit git checkout of package-lock.json — Plan 05 deploy gate refuses to run on a dirty tree; a reformatted lockfile with an identical dependency set would block a deploy for no real reason
- [Phase 01]: Playwright tests build/ served by wrangler dev through the real Worker, never vite preview; the harness choice is written into playwright.config.ts with its measured cold start (4.6 s) — Kit preview boots the Node server from .svelte-kit/output/server and never reads build/, so sv's generated harness validated an artifact that is not the deployed one; wrangler dev serves the deployed bytes and makes the Basic Auth gate a regression test
- [Phase 01]: The unauthenticated gate assertion uses Node fetch, not Playwright's request API — Both Playwright routes were observed returning 200 where 401 was expected: an explicit httpCredentials undefined in test.use reads as "not specified" and falls back to defineConfig, and a context from the module-level request API picks the same credentials up under the runner
- [Phase 01]: vite.config.ts prerender.handleHttpError suppresses a 404 for exactly /LICENSE, /THIRD-PARTY.md and /source-*.tar.gz and rethrows everything else — scripts/postbuild.mjs writes those three after vite build, so the prerenderer crawling the footer links cannot see them; a global suppression would have silently accepted a genuinely broken internal link
- [Phase 01]: Killing a stray wrangler dev requires killing the whole process tree (npx-cli to wrangler.js to wrangler-dist/cli.js to workerd.exe); killing workerd alone leaves a listener on 4173 that accepts and never answers — cli.js restarts workerd when it dies; the resulting zombie makes every curl hang until its timeout and Playwright's reuseExistingServer would happily attach to it
- [Phase 01]: The deploy is one Node script with six refusing gates and no bypass flag; the clean-tree gate exits before the build because git archive ships HEAD while Vite bundles the working tree, so a dirty deploy would serve an archive that is not the source of the bundle beside it
- [Phase 01]: Worker secrets are set AFTER the first deploy, not before — wrangler secret put targets a Worker that does not exist until an upload creates one, and the fail-closed gate turns the 29-second gap into a 401-to-everything state rather than a window of public exposure
- [Phase 01]: CLAUDE.md is regenerated with gsd-tools generate-claude-md, never hand-edited — its GSD:project block is rendered from PROJECT.md, so a hand edit silently reverts on the next regeneration and re-introduces the public-source claim D-02 contradicts
- [Phase 03]: D-10 fired on measurement: the invariant sweep is 38.8 s wall against 6.5 s for everything else, so pad-invariants.test.js runs as its own Vitest 'sweep' project per wave while 'test:quick' stays the per-task loop — A separate project, not a skip or an env flag, so the sweep is never partially run; and it is excluded from the server project by FILE NAME, never by directory, because a directory-wide vendor exclusion is precisely the failure this task removed
- [Phase 03]: The vendored tree is hidden from svelte-check with a tsconfig exclude of src/vendor/**, not by turning checkJs off — Both give 0 errors (489 without either), but checkJs:false silently lowers the bar for every future first-party HANGAR .js file; the exclude keeps checkJs and still type-checks the three vendored .ts sources the moment HANGAR code imports them, which is where the real risk is
- [Phase 03]: Provenance headers are 7 lines for _pad.ts and 6 for the other five; VENDOR.md records the resulting 8/7 line offset and the rule 'vendored line = upstream line + header lines' — The block shifts every line number in the file, so every HANGAR document citing a vendored line cites the upstream number plus the offset; verified against a real citation - PRESETS is upstream _pad.ts:4206 and vendored _pad.ts:4214
- [Phase 03]: Cross-repo vendoring is a Buffer-level copy with a uniqueness assertion per permitted delta, never a decode/re-encode round trip through a string — pad.test.js is UTF-8 with non-ASCII content and plan 02's sha256 manifest hashes its exact byte stream; a Buffer.indexOf/concat splice that throws on zero or multiple matches also proves 'exactly three deltas' at copy time rather than after the fact
- [Phase 03]: D-04 is enforced by hash, not by prose: upstream-manifest.json pins the pristine upstream sha256 and byte length of all six vendored BOTOR files at a0fb69d5, and vendored-diff.spec.ts reconstructs those bytes from each vendored copy — A 1 KB hash manifest asserts exactly the property a committed second copy would assert, without duplicating 436 KB in the repository and again inside every per-deploy GPLv3 source archive. The five inverse deltas are the D-04 allow-list in executable form, so an unrecorded fourth change fails to reconstruct instead of being absorbed.
- [Phase 03]: The fidelity suite reads nothing outside the repository, so it is green on a machine with no grid-editor checkout; format-parity.spec.ts stays the one deliberate sibling-dependent canary — A gate that cannot run on a fresh clone is not a gate. The spec asserts mechanically that no executable line mentions grid-editor, git-common-dir or BOTOR_REPO. The header block is stripped through its sentinel rather than by a fixed line count, so plan 01 per-file offsets never enter the comparison and a future header edit cannot silently shift a delta out of the hashed region.
- [Phase 03]: The preset baseline records FOUR lengths per preset - setup/timer raw and setup/timer compressed - plus both cost().used values, and the D-11 bump gate asserts the COMPRESSED pair — cost().used is max(compressed, raw) + reserved, and measured on live data compressed is exactly one character shorter than raw for all nine presets in both events - so cost().used IS the raw length everywhere. A grid-protocol bump whose minifier spent five more characters would move compressScript(...).length and leave cost().used untouched, and a gate recording only cost would stay green through precisely the change it exists to catch.
- [Phase 03]: The fixture is captured by running BOTOR's OWN compiler inside the sibling checkout, imported by absolute file:// URL with cwd staying in HANGAR, awaiting the SIBLING's padCompilerReady() — Module resolution crosses the repository boundary by itself, so the import binds grid-protocol to grid-editor's own node_modules without moving cwd - and keeping cwd in HANGAR is what guarantees no bundler cache can ever be created next door. HANGAR's copy of the protocol package is a DIFFERENT module instance: readying HANGAR's formatter leaves the imported compiler's cost() throwing "The Lua formatter is not initialised.", a failure that reads as a WASM packaging problem and is not one. The fixture being produced by the original is what makes the criterion-3 spec evidence rather than a tautology.
- [Phase 03]: protocol-pin.spec.ts measures with GridScript.compressScript directly rather than through the vendored measure() — The pin gate is about the installed package. Routing it through the vendored compiler would make it fail for reasons that belong to preset-baseline.spec.ts, and would make it unrunnable while the vendored copy is mid-resync.
- [Phase 03]: docs/PIN-POLICY.md's bump checklist names commands and counts (test:quick 176 + 96, test:sweep 9, the two spec files) instead of describing a property — "The vendored suite is green" cannot rot. A checklist item that names a command and its expected count stops being runnable the moment it stops being true, which is the point of a checklist a human follows before moving a pin.
- [Phase 03]: The oracle spec honours ORACLE.LED_LOOKUP_DIRECTION as declared and never tries the inverse; the ZONA table is self-inverse, so the 81-cell agreement confirms the DATA independently but cannot confirm the DIRECTION — LED_LOOKUP[LED_LOOKUP[i]] === i for all 81 entries, so both readings produce identical numbers and a spec that tried both directions until one passed would be fitting the oracle to the simulator - the exact failure mode the two-author design exists to prevent. The direction rests on the isolated author's derivation from grid_led.c:171-183 plus grid_lua_api.c:1301-1336, recorded in 03-04-SUMMARY.md so no reviewer mistakes numeric agreement for confirmation of it.
- [Phase 03]: The expiry tick order is asserted through the vendored public API alone - defaultState() with look and sends disabled decays to animating false at tick 42 - and the oracle's phaseAdvanceOffsetAtExpiry INDEXES the expected frame rather than being restated in the spec — The settled frame at expiry+1 is compared against hashes[expiry-1+offset], so flipping the oracle constant flips the expectation and the test goes red - observed. The spec also asserts hashes[expiry-1] !== hashes[expiry], without which the offset would be vacuously satisfied. rateZeroedOnExpiry is asserted the same observable way: movedAfterExpiry === !rateZeroedOnExpiry. The plan's weaker static-preset fallback was not needed and 03-VALIDATION.md's "if the fallback fires" row stands unfired.
- [Phase 03]: Golden-frame regeneration lives inside the spec behind UPDATE_GOLDEN, shells out to npx prettier --write on the fixture, and fails the run by design; the fixture is git added the moment it first exists, before any perturbation — Plain Node ESM cannot import pad-sim.ts at all, so a standalone generator would need Vite anyway. JSON.stringify puts the primitive ticks array on five lines and Prettier collapses it onto one, and src/lib/fidelity/ is not prettier-ignored, so without the normalising pass npm run lint fails on a file no human wrote - and the pass being idempotent is what keeps two consecutive regenerations byte-identical. Staging first matters because on an untracked path git checkout -- fails outright and git diff --quiet passes vacuously, so every restore-and-compare check would have measured nothing.
- [Phase 03]: The FOUND-05 WASM gate attaches to HANGAR's COMPILE SURFACE (src/lib/pad/index.ts), never to the app root; PadSim is not re-exported from that surface at all — CONTEXT says the app root awaits the gate before first render of anything that compiles and CLAUDE.md says never call initLuaFormatter() at boot - both hold, because nothing calls padReady() until something asks for a cost, and what a page shows while that resolves is Phase 4's concern. Leaving PadSim out of the barrel entirely (a consumer imports src/vendor/botor/pad-sim directly) makes "the simulator is outside the gate" enforceable by grep on the import graph rather than by a comment nobody re-reads; gating it would make the catalog wait on a 628 KB WASM download for a picture that takes a PadState and never Lua.
- [Phase 03]: In a spec whose test ORDER is load-bearing, a pre-init assertion must be the FIRST gate-crossing call in the file - ready.spec.ts test 3 builds with the VENDORED compile, not compilePreset — As the plan drafted it, test 3 opened with await compilePreset("aurora"), which awaits the gate - so by the time costOf ran the formatter was already initialised, and deleting costOf's own await left the whole spec green (exit 0, 5 passed). The plan's negative check found this, which is exactly what a negative check is for. compile() needs no formatter (test 1 proves it in the same file), so building with the vendored compile makes costOf the first call to cross the gate and the perturbation goes red naming test 3. Corollary recorded in the spec: compilePreset's and compileState's own awaits are belt-and-braces and unobservable by ANY test, because compile() never touches the formatter - the four load-bearing gates are costOf, fitsIn, measureLua and validateCompiled.
- [Phase 03]: The D-12 browser probe compiles and costs through $lib/pad rather than the vendored compiler, from a dynamic await import inside onMount, and never at module scope — At module scope the SERVER build resolves @wasm-fmt/lua_fmt through its "node" export condition and reads the wasm off disk during prerender, which proves nothing about a browser. Going through $lib/pad rather than the vendored functions makes the run exercise HANGAR's own FOUND-05 gate against the deployed artifact, not just grid-protocol's packaging. Measured: aurora's seven recorded numbers reproduced in Chromium in 1.0 s on a cold wrangler, empty console.
- [Phase 03]: worker/index.js sets NO Content-Security-Policy header, and any CSP added later MUST include 'wasm-unsafe-eval' in script-src; e2e/fidelity.e2e.ts asserts the wasm response is application/wasm — Captured live: the wasm asset returns exactly seven headers (200, Content-Length 628148, Content-Type application/wasm, Cache-Control private no-store, ETag, CF-Cache-Status, Referrer-Policy, X-Robots-Tag) and no CSP. Without 'wasm-unsafe-eval' a future CSP breaks instantiation with a symptom indistinguishable from "the formatter never initialised". A WRONG MIME is worse than a missing one: @wasm-fmt/lua_fmt falls back from instantiateStreaming to WebAssembly.instantiate with only a console warning and then runs the slow path forever, which is why the MIME is asserted rather than assumed.
- [Phase 03]: Playwright output is captured into the gitignored .tmp-e2e/, never under test-results/ or playwright-report/, and no Playwright test title may contain the word failed — Playwright deletes its outputDir (default test-results/) at the start of every run, so a redirect target inside it is unlinked mid-run and any grep over it reads a path that no longer exists. The gate asserts an exact passed total AND the absence of the word failed, because "N passed" also appears in a partly-failing run - which in turn makes a test title containing that word a permanent false negative. .tmp-format-parity/ established the convention; .tmp-e2e/ sits beside it in .gitignore.
- [Phase 02]: descriptors.ts is the ONLY shipped module that calls encode_packet, and forbidden-instructions.spec.ts proves it by scanning src/lib/protocol and src/lib/transport rather than by convention — Convention would not survive plan 03 page or plan 02 transport. The same spec assembles its forbidden needles from fragments (["NVM","ERASE"].join("")) so the spec source does not contain the literals it forbids and a future rule could scan it without an exclusion.
- [Phase 02]: matchResponse coerces both sides through Number and compares strictly, so a filter key the incoming class does not carry becomes NaN and fails — The desktop loose != existed because its decoder produced strings. The NaN consequence is why the write filter names no class parameters at all: a CONFIG/ACKNOWLEDGE is a six-byte class block whose PAGENUMBER, EVENTTYPE and ACTIONSTRING are all undefined.
- [Phase 02]: match.spec builds every incoming class from real encoder output and then rewrites ONLY SX/SY to 0 — encode_packet forces the source address to zero on the wire and the decoder subtracts 127, so an outbound frame decodes SX -127 while a directly attached module reports SX 0. Rewriting the two address fields keeps the class shape genuine decoder output instead of an invented literal.
- [Phase 02]: The framing spec EOT-boundary test splits at len-4, not the plan and research len-3 — A frame ends EOT c c LF, so EOT sits at len-4; slicing at len-3 leaves EOT as the last byte of the FIRST chunk and makes the test own name false. len-3 is covered anyway by the every-split-point test.
- [Phase 02]: A NACK's rejection message is deliberately kept OUT of the vendored TRANSIENT_WRITE pattern, and queue.spec test 8 asserts the timeout and the abort match while the NACK does not — TRANSIENT_WRITE is the predicate Phase 7's withRetry uses to decide what to retry, and firmware NACKs a CONFIG/EXECUTE only for deterministic reasons - an ACTIONLENGTH that does not land on the ETX, a page that is not active, an element that does not exist (grid_decode.c:1260-1313). A busy store is dropped silently and surfaces as a timeout, not a NACK. Making the NACK match would make Phase 7 retry a refusal, contradicting the plan's own must-have that a negative acknowledgement is never retried.
- [Phase 02]: The frame pump lives OUTSIDE RequestQueue: the page wires transport.onData through FrameScanner and decodeFrame into queue.deliver, and transport.onClose into queue.abort — The plan's own doc comment says deliver is "called by the frame pump", and GridTransport carries a single onData callback - so a queue that registered it would own the only tap on the byte stream and D-07's recorder could never see chunks or refused frames, which are exactly the events the queue never receives. queue.spec's pump() helper is six lines and is plan 03's reference implementation.
- [Phase 02]: fixtures/synthetic.spec.ts regenerates at MODULE scope and throws, with no guard test, deliberately diverging from golden-frames.spec.ts; and CaptureRecorder gained a capturedAt override — golden-frames.spec.ts carries a guard it() that counts in the totals; a guarded variant here would need a skipIf and a skipped count would shift every aggregate total stated in plans 02 through 05 and in 02-VALIDATION.md, all written with no skip allowance. The safety property is preserved: setting UPDATE_SYNTHETIC rewrites, normalises with Prettier, then fails the run. capturedAt is pinned by the generator because otherwise two consecutive regenerations differ by one line and reviewing the diff of the other 32 events is pointless.
- [Phase 02]: FakeTransport emits live responses SYNCHRONOUSLY inside write(), and every timing-sensitive queue assertion is driven by an explicit delay fault rather than by scheduler ordering — It makes the disconnect test genuine: the close callback fires DURING the write, before the queue reaches its await, which is the real race an unplug creates - and is why arm() attaches a no-op catch to the waiter's promise the moment it is created. It also removes the flake from "one outstanding request", where with no fault the first request settles inside a microtask and there is no window in which to observe the second write being held.
- [Phase 02]: The frame pump lives in the page and records before it dispatches: onData to recorder.rxChunk to FrameScanner to decodeFrame to recorder.rxFrame to absorbFrame to queue.deliver, identity offered before the queue — GridTransport carries a single onData, so whoever registers it owns the only tap on the byte stream. Putting the pump in the page keeps D-07's recorder in the loop for chunks and for refused frames - the events the queue never receives - and the queue writes through a thin recording wrapper so outbound frames, including retries, are on the record too. queue.spec.ts's six-line pump() was the reference implementation.
- [Phase 02]: D-10 is a three-condition rule, not a field read: the active page moves only when the class carries a PAGENUMBER, a HEARTBEAT rode in the SAME decoded frame, and the class carries neither EVENTTYPE nor ACTIONLENGTH — A CONFIG/REPORT also carries a PAGENUMBER and one arrives on every single fetch, so a rule that read the field alone would silently retarget the whole run at whatever page was last fetched. sequence.spec test 9 feeds a report naming page 5 to a run identified on page 2 and asserts the page does not move. The whole spec runs on active page 2 and the scripted responder answers any other page with an empty string, so a regression that reached for a constant is caught by D-09's guard rather than passing quietly.
- [Phase 02]: The restore heartbeat is held in three independent places: runNoOpCycle's finally, the page's Write back button's own finally, and a standing Restore button gated on nothing but the port being open — The page drives the cycle from separate clicks (D-11: nothing is written without a click) and therefore never calls runNoOpCycle, so the shared function's finally would not have protected a real hardware write. The deleted-finally mutation was observed turning sequence.spec test 7 red naming the last frame's class as CONFIG rather than HEARTBEAT - which is exactly the state that leaves the user's module unable to change page until it is power-cycled.
- [Phase 02]: The 300 ms keeper heartbeat is recorded as tx bytes but suppressed from the capture's steps; PROTOCOL_PIN is re-exported through src/lib/protocol/constants.ts so the page can record the pin without a third import name — A 60 second run at 300 ms would put two hundred restore-page-change steps in the capture and bury the eight transactions docs/SKELETON-RESULTS.md is written from. And CaptureRun.protocolPin is required while D-05 caps the page at svelte, $lib/protocol and $lib/transport, which config-shape.spec.ts test 10 now asserts mechanically - the pin literal stays in src/lib/protocol-pin.ts where three assertions hold it against package.json and the lockfile.
- [Phase 02]: Page copy whose exact numbers are asserted lives in a script constant, and the degrade e2e waits for the panel before counting the connect control — Prettier reflowed the falsifiable heartbeat definition in markup and split 1000 from ms across a line break, which would have made the page's most load-bearing sentence formatter-dependent; as a string it cannot be reflowed. And getByTestId().count() takes a snapshot without auto-waiting while the page decides Web Serial availability in onMount, so a count taken before hydration would read zero whether or not the connect control would eventually render - the precise 'passes for the wrong reason' failure the precondition assertion exists to avoid.
- [Phase 02]: The host heartbeat is NOT required: arm B ran with hostHeartbeat.enabled false and the module sent 1,086 inbound HEARTBEAT frames over 271.5 s (4.00/s, dead steady) while every CONFIG/EXECUTE and PAGESTORE/EXECUTE was still acknowledged - the phase's LOW-confidence open question is closed, and the restore rule is unaffected because a successful CONFIG/EXECUTE clears page_change_enabled whether or not the host was beating
- [Phase 02]: The 10 ms pre-send pacing is NOT load-bearing at 2 Mbaud: the same twenty-fetch burst measured min 13.8 / p50 14.5 / max 16.5 ms at pace 10 and min 3.0 / p50 3.3 / max 3.8 ms at pace 0, with zero timeouts and zero NACKs on both - roughly 11 ms per request for nothing. Recommendation for plan 05 carries the caveat that this is one module, one cable, one host, and that Phase 7 writes far more data per request than a fetch
- [Phase 02]: The real ZONA reported activePage 3, not 0, and its factory Setup is 642 characters where the pinned package's default table says 641 - D-10's read-the-page rule is vindicated on hardware (a page-0 assumption would have fetched an empty string), and the module's own string is the truth while the package default is only a hint, which any Phase 7 budget or diff logic has to assume
- [Phase 02]: Chrome 152 keeps the Web Serial grant across a full browser restart: after quitting Chrome completely and reopening it, the page read 'previously granted ports: 1' for http://127.0.0.1:4173, so navigator.serial.getPorts() returns the ZONA with no gesture and no chooser and Phase 6's CONN-06 silent-reconnect offer is viable - the grant is per origin, and a returned port is permitted rather than open
- [Phase 02]: The shipped timeouts are the measured ones: fetchMs 300, executeMs 250, pagestoreMs 3000 (measured and unchanged), PRE_SEND_DELAY_MS 0 — Ten times the slowest thing ever observed on each fast path, from 160 real requests that all settled under 40 ms. pagestoreMs keeps its headroom because a PAGESTORE arriving during a bulk NVM operation is dropped with no ACK and no NACK, so a timeout is its only signal. A test parses one machine-readable Shipped: line out of docs/SKELETON-RESULTS.md and asserts it equals the constants, so the two cannot drift.
- [Phase 02]: A committed capture declares its provenance, and fixtures.spec.ts fails when the repository holds only synthetic evidence — Every fixture-backed test before the hardware checkpoint ran against synthetic-zona.json, which is what let the whole protocol and transport layer be built with nothing plugged in. Test 1 of src/lib/transport/fixtures/fixtures.spec.ts closes that gap: it reads every JSON in the fixtures directory and fails unless one declares source hardware. Tests 2 to 4 select the arms by filename so the provenance mutation makes exactly that gate red.
- [Phase 02]: DESKTOP_PRE_SEND_DELAY_MS keeps the skeleton page A/B pacing toggle honest now that the shipped default is 0 — The page stamps the capture run.id from the pacing number, so reading the shipped 0 in the paced position would have sent at 0 in both toggle states and labelled every future export pace-0 - a capture that lies about which arm produced it. Nothing in the shipped request path reads the new constant.
- [Phase 02]: The restore rule is asserted as one restore-page-change per write-setup per arm, not as a closing step — The pace-0 probe is identify plus one burst and never wrote a config, so it correctly has no restore. Asserting the invariant as restores.length === writes.length per arm, plus at least one arm having exercised it, is a stronger claim than the closing-step version and passes honestly on all three captures instead of exempting one.
- [Phase 08]: restsBlack is a per-entry declared fact proved by frames.json in both directions (D-19), not a hard-coded darkness exemption; a growing catalog cannot carry a literal list
- [Phase 08]: CatalogEntry.defaults holds knob INDICES, never values (D-13), and LuaKnob.kind draws on the vendored KnobKind vocabulary rather than a Lua-specific union (D-12)
- [Phase 08]: src/lib/catalog/ names neither the pinned protocol package nor the compile surface: the three constants are literals asserted in the spec, and build() takes compiled Lua as an argument, so a page that only lists names never pulls in the WASM-gated chunk
- [Phase 08]: scripts/check-counts.mjs replaces every literal suite total (D-17): counts are an observed baseline plus a stated delta, since Phase 4 and Phase 8 interleave in one tree
- [Phase 04]: OFL-1.1 added to the gen-licenses allowlist as a deliberate policy extension: Quicksand ships as separate static woff2 assets beside the GPLv3 bundle, never linked into it, and the build output proves it
- [Phase 04]: The identity spec normalises numbers before comparing token values, because Prettier rewrites 0.50 to 0.5 in CSS and the spec must encode 04-UI-SPEC rather than the formatter's output
- [Phase 04]: The 9x9 favicon is inlined into build/index.html as a data URI, not emitted as an asset file: at 3,651 bytes it sits under Vite's 4,096-byte threshold, so build assertions read index.html
- [Phase 04]: The front-door opening window promises no dark pad, three animated at the largest slots and no two quiet pads adjacent - not D-20's motion-only, which seven visible slots of eight make arithmetically impossible without faking motion
- [Phase 04]: Every coverflow geometry number comes from one pure function of the signed ring distance in src/lib/coverflow/slots.ts, so the ladder is tested in node rather than inside a component no Vitest project collects
- [Phase 04]: The engine-facing id in TouchTarget is the contact SLOT (0..4), not the pointer id: the vendored deliverPending passes c.slot, so the host in 04-05 must not pass a pointer id there
- [Phase 04]: TouchSampler.down() returns false for an already-tracked pointer as well as for a full slot table, matching the vendored previewDown's early return - a duplicate pointerdown would otherwise leak a slot forever
- [Phase 04]: failureCopy takes a trailing, defaulted controlLabel (D-23): the label is the THIRD parameter, after raw, so the three existing positional callers and all six Phase 2 assertions are untouched
- [Phase 04]: The never-writes invariant is asserted twice - FakeTransport.writes empty after a full cycle, and a comment-stripped scan of try-on.ts for .write( - and was proven with two separate perturbations, because a Vitest assertion aborts its test at the first failure
- [Phase 04]: SimHost asks for another animation frame only for pads still running after their ticks, not for every pad that ran: the expiry frame has already painted the true final state and every waking event calls wake(), so the vendored host's extra no-op frame is dead work
- [Phase 04]: HostEngine is declared structurally in host.ts rather than imported, so PadSim today and Phase 8 plan 08-03's SimEngine both satisfy it with no shared file, no import either way and no migration
- [Phase 04]: The pad's four layers are one canvas plus three static CSS layers: PadCanvas owns the element and its role=img name but never takes a 2D context, sets a backing store or paints - SimHost.register does all three, so exactly one place decides the 9x9 store and there is one draw call per pad per paint
- [Phase 04]: The CSS 3D scene is split across three elements: clip and edge mask on the outer .band (no preserve-3d), perspective on the inner .stage, filter: brightness() on the leaf .slot - measured in Chromium at 1280x720 the row renders mirror-symmetric about x=640 with the hero 374px and the +-3 slots 138px, so the ladder is provably not flattened
